   - Neighbors of G: B, J, O

3. **Identify all paths between N and G**:
   - Path 1: N -> C -> B -> G
   - Path 2: N -> B -> G
   - Path 3: N -> C -> B -> J -> G
   - Path 4: N -> C -> B -> O -> Q -> P -> H -> I -> J -> G
   - Path 5: N -> C -> B -> O -> R -> S -> E -> F -> T -> H -> I -> J -> G

4. **Find bottlenecks in the paths**:
   - Node B is a common bottleneck in multiple paths.
   - Node C is a bottleneck in multiple paths.
   - Node J is a bottleneck in multiple paths.

5. **Evaluate the found paths**:
   - Removing B, C, and J will disconnect all paths between N and G.

**Check the answer**:
   - Removing B, C, and J will ensure that N and G are completely disconnected.

   - The black node P is directly connected to node Q.
   - The black node R is directly connected to nodes C and O.
   - By following the connections, we see:
     - P -> Q -> J -> I -> H -> G -> C -> R

     - P -> Q -> J -> I -> H -> G -> B -> T -> F -> E -> A -> S -> B -> C -> R
     - P -> Q -> J -> G -> C -> R
     - P -> Q -> O -> R

   - Q connects to P and has multiple branches (J, O)
   - R connects directly to C and O
   - Removing (Q, P) disconnects P.
   - Removing (C, R) and (O, R) fully disconnects R (experimenting with paths still keeping one of these would still connect through J).

3. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - To dissect all connection paths between P and R, the following edges must be removed:
     - Removing both (P, Q) and (R, C) and (R, O).

   - From F to C through T to B to N: F -> T -> B -> C -> N
   - From F to C through T to B to R: F -> T -> B -> C -> R

   - Removing edge (F, T) will break the path F -> T -> B -> C.

   Hence, removing the edges (F, B), (T, B), and (B, C) ensures that F and C are completely disconnected.
